# Source-first independent release expectation intake

Task: derive a finite qualification matrix for the authorized STDO 3.1.0-rc.1 release from the governing sources below BEFORE receiving candidate implementations, indexes, test outputs, author expectations or verdicts. This is expectation derivation only; no candidate result is sought at this stage. Root's exact activation is included verbatim.

Requested review relation: six separately owned Products coordinated by the release activation. Every Product has its own definition and verified installed 3.0.4 construction basis; there is no synthetic seventh Product or implicit adoption. Product/status bindings below are mechanical source verification, not semantic verdicts.

Authority: the Root release activation explicitly permits review operators to retain actual independent-family Claude results and write only their reports. You return through this print session; the authorized assessment recorder persists stdout unchanged. No candidate or other writes are permitted. Use the installed stdo-review instructions below in this source-first stage. Final exact candidate identities will be supplied in a later stage.

Acquisition: this packet contains verbatim governing-source projections with exact original line ranges and digests. No candidate implementation, generated index body, previous expected response, or candidate test result is included. Source Product documents may refer to historic standing; such source prose is not supplied evidence of a later candidate. All tools are disabled for this expectation stage; request any genuinely missing material source by exact path/section in your return. The packet is sufficient for expectation derivation only, not a candidate verdict. No hostile-code or clean-room isolation claim is made.

Authorship and host: the operator is Codex and a release assessment recorder, not the Product source author. You are a separate Claude first-party model family, selected through a fresh CLI print session with safe mode, no tools, no inherited conversation, and no session persistence. CLI metadata supplies actual model/session, not this request's assertion. No semantic actor-family waiver is selected. Do not infer independence solely from fresh context; declare the available authorship/evidence conditions and residuals.

Output: (1) source-derived minimal release predicates with source citations, exact observation boundary and finite falsifier; (2) semantic/behavior/migration case matrix for the changed Purpose Context/default SDLC/ticket-context/Skills/visual-only path, preserving cross-Product interactions; (3) prior-evidence applicability criteria, mandatory reruns and allowed reuse; (4) gaps or ambiguous governing obligations requiring a declared disposition before final claims; (5) exact evidence needed for your later candidate assessment. Keep historical duties on their historical subjects. Do not add universal review rounds, require a general whole-method reliability proof for bounded release claims, revive retired runtime requirements without governing authority, or invent supported coverage. Those are source interpretation questions for your independent judgment, not expected answers. Return observations, scope and limits; do not authorize publication, assign Product priority or supply a release verdict.

# Repository bootstrap

This root coordinates six Products; it is not a Product. Read the current
[development inventory](DEVELOPMENT.md), then select exactly one Product scope or
an explicit cross-Product relation. STDO has two source version lines under
specification_methodology/v2 and specification_methodology/v3; they count as one
Product. Purpose Context, STDO Skills, Representation Tools (a_c), STDO
Representation and Axiom Indexer retain independent Product definitions.

Read the selected child's AGENTS.md, README, specification/GOALS.md,
specification/INTENT.md, specification/PRODUCT.md and applicable requirements/design.
Resolve its exact Product Definition and installed methodology basis. Mutable
sibling source, a symbolic index, shared actor or matching output supplies no
implicit adoption, composition, semantic authority or effect grant.

Purpose Context owns reusable purpose indexes and context construction. The
root `$stdo-context` route is the [thin Skills adapter](stdo_skills/skills/stdo-context/SKILL.md)
that verifies the selected independent Product and follows its canonical skill.
Use matching existing indexes for relevant task/build/review/refresh constraints;
preserve unknown facts, source re-entry, exact purpose and coverage limits.
Source-first independent review derives obligations before candidate indexes.

Representation Tools and STDO Representation serve visual documentation. No
active root axiomatize-corpus, stdo-representation, context registry or formal
support workflow remains. Optional visual selection does not become a context
prerequisite.

The Executive coordinates dependency-ready work and consumes closed results.
Separately granted Writers perform exact scoped file/Git effects. Existing owner
authorization persists; routines do not require repeated approval. Reviewers
assess exact closed subjects without repairing their own assessment targets.

Current packages are explicitly unversioned development source with separate
Product manifests. No successor release is declared qualified. The root
stack_release.json and archived five-Product constructor/test families are
historical contracts only. Preserve immutable releases, original evidence,
installed/global files and unrelated work. Do not run a historical release gate
as the current source acceptance check.

# STDO 3 Source Instructions

Current source distribution navigation is [development/TOOLS.md](development/TOOLS.md).
The former common-tools constructor family is historical and archived. It is not
a current implementation or a successor release gate. The exact installed native
method and its owning standards retain their existing selection and authority.


This scope is the peer source project located by [stdo_3.json](stdo_3.json). Read its exact installed STDO basis, [Product](specification/PRODUCT.md), [Goals](specification/GOALS.md), [frame basis](specification/REFERENCE_FRAME_BASIS.md) and applicable work grant before effects.

Jim owns the common WHAT. Codex holds its construction pen under [T-001](.ai-workspace/tickets/active/T-001-common-definition-and-checkpoints.md). Common changes receive review independent of their author before Jim accepts them; checkpoint commits provide review visibility. Reviews are written under `.ai-workspace/comments/<reviewer>/`. Working principles and tentative capability questions do not alter the installed builder law or become accepted through presence.

Historical C3 common input is C3-I5-06 at `4c340f7adec9f3c893e3e267b23e4cadeb357854`, ratified in [T-001](.ai-workspace/tickets/active/T-001-common-definition-and-checkpoints.md#iteration-5-ratification). Codex's I5 construction is admitted in its [tenant ticket](.ai-workspace/tickets/active/T-002-CODEX-c3-successor-construction.md#current-iteration-5-contract). Each line retains its own candidate and admission; no Claude activation follows from the Codex instruction. [T-002](.ai-workspace/tickets/active/T-002-c3-successor-construction.md#iteration-5-consolidated-work) retains that reconstruction contract. Current work follows [Goals](specification/GOALS.md) and its exact new grant; the historical admission does not activate future work. New admissions bind the exact ratified input, own preimage, selected donor, actors, limits and exit proof; historical admissions and immutable source records remain preserved.

Tenant implementations, wording and a_c encodings are HOW; neither tenant can redefine shared meaning or select itself as canonical. Tenant work follows the [tenant entry contract](build_tenants/README.md), [Cross-Synthesis](specification/PRODUCT.md#cross-synthesis) and [Development Isolation](specification/PRODUCT.md#development-isolation): own branch, own sparse worktree, own territory. Peer reads use the exact authorised donor or review commit. A common checkpoint makes a proposal reviewable; it accepts no tenant member, replacement, candidate, merge or release.

An Executive coordinates and decides only inside its grant. It does not mutate files or Git. A separately declared Writer performs effects in exact granted territory. Independent source-first reviewers inspect a frozen subject, do not repair it and cannot assess their own authorship.

Preserve all sibling source roots, current cohort, prior posts and unrelated work. This source project now adopts the released 3.0.4 builder by the owner instruction recorded in [the adoption intake](.ai-workspace/work/STDO304_BUILDER_ADOPTION/INTAKE.json). No peer adoption or new release is implied.

## Current Builder And Tools

Use [the versioned binding and guide](development/STDO_3_0_4.md) for the exact `stdo://releases/v3.0.4/` native plugin, compact `read_basis.py` helper and bundled tool sources. Verify `stdo_3.json`, enter `standards/frames/bootstrap.md`, then explicitly load the applicable installed `plugins/spec/skills/stdo-*/SKILL.md` with its relative references intact. This is exact file loading, not host registration. The ignored `.stdo/tools/3.0.4/` tree is the verified writable source materialization. Mutable sibling source and the archive's independent older Skills packages do not supply the current native workflow.

The [current frame configuration](specification/REFERENCE_FRAME_BASIS.md#activations-and-owners) applies to future work. C3 contracts, predecessor claims and qualification remain historical on their original 2.5.1-rc.2 basis; adopting 3.0.4 does not classify or qualify legacy operations under its deterministic-operation rules.

<!-- STDO_BOOTSTRAP_START -->
## STDO Bootstrap

This scope is routed by an STDO Product Definition Overlay.

Before constitutional work:

1. Resolve the applicable `stdo_<label>.json` for the requested Product scope.
2. Use `constitution.stdo.basis`, not its mutable selector, as the operative basis.
3. Resolve and verify that exact installed release through the STDO toolchain manager.
4. Load the Product Definition's declared bootstrap entrypoint, then exact owning standards as needed.
5. Resolve the applicable accepted Project Reference-Frame Basis or its declared composition.
6. Bind the exact outcome, basis and existing work grant. Use direct Writer entry when the accepted frame configuration permits it and one capable context suffices; use Executive or the declared project equivalent for material coordination. Activate only applicable dependency-ready evaluations, preserving unknown applicability and required independence. Executive does not mutate files or Git; a separate Writer activation binds those effects.
7. Fail closed when the Product Definition, frame basis, subject, authority, or activation is missing, ambiguous, stale, or outside the governed scope.

Mutable methodology source, another installed version, a cache entry, and this
bootstrap cannot replace the exact basis selected by the Product Definition.
A prompt, summary, symbolic map, or prior result may route attention but cannot
replace current source authority or a closed frame result.
<!-- STDO_BOOTSTRAP_END -->

===== END SOURCE =====

# Specification Methodology

This source project develops Specification Methodology. Its current builder is the exact installed **STDO 3.0.4** selected by [stdo_3.json](stdo_3.json), under the owner's [adoption instruction](.ai-workspace/work/STDO304_BUILDER_ADOPTION/INTAKE.json). [The versioned binding and guide](development/STDO_3_0_4.md) pins the released native workflow, compact source reader and bundled tools. The original two-tenant C3 reconstruction and its evidence retain their 2.5.1-rc.2 basis; the 2.* source and peer Products remain separate.

Current source packages follow the separate [development tool owners](development/TOOLS.md).
The previous five-Product distribution constructors are archived; they do not
construct or qualify the current six-Product source arrangement. The publication
records below describe their original released subjects, not current source bytes.

## Start with the outcome

1. Resolve [stdo_3.json](stdo_3.json), verify its exact installed basis and enter
   the [3.0.4 native workflow](development/STDO_3_0_4.md). Read current
   [Goals](specification/GOALS.md), [Intent](specification/INTENT.md) and
   [Product](specification/PRODUCT.md) for the requested outcome and existing grant.
2. Use the [selected SDLC default](specification/REFERENCE_FRAME_BASIS.md#selected-sdlc-default):
   work → incremental QA → causal triage → owner re-entry → affected verification
   → continue or close. The [guide](build_tenants/common/standards/SDLC_DEFAULTS.md)
   and [worked cases](build_tenants/common/standards/examples/SDLC_DEFAULTS.md)
   distinguish ordinary repair closure from named acceptance, qualification and
   release. A sufficient direct Writer needs no added coordinator or ticket;
   applicable independent-review conditions still hold.
3. For host setup, follow the independent [Skills installation](../../stdo_skills/scripts/INSTALLATION.md)
   and [first-use guide](../../stdo_skills/GETTING_STARTED.md). Select Purpose
   Context's `sdlc-workflow/1` for workflow decisions, or the exact applicable
   `python-pure-refactor/2` entry for its narrower construction constraints.
   Supply actual query facts, consume and check the delivered context, then
   return to the native work/review skill and Product-owned decision.
4. For tenant work, use the [tenant entry contract](build_tenants/README.md).
   Existing C3 admissions retain their historical subjects; they do not start
   new tenant work.

The [current native Getting Started source](build_tenants/common/plugins/spec/references/GETTING_STARTED.md)
explains the work loop and separate setup choices. The new default and native
work/review guidance are development source with bounded
[delivery evidence](../../.ai-workspace/work/SDLC_DEFAULTS_2026-11-14/DELIVERY.md).
This Product explicitly selected the default projection in its frame basis;
the [template](build_tenants/common/standards/templates/PROJECT_REFERENCE_FRAME_BASIS_TEMPLATE.md#sdlc-default-selection)
offers it for another Product's authorized adoption. Finding the guide or
installing Skills does not adopt it. The workflow index has 35 rules over 12
exact source members, separate from the refactoring index over released
3.0.5-rc.1 source and the installed 3.0.4 builder. No 3.0.6 release is declared.

Skills installation, exact-query context coverage, actual task success and
release qualification each need their own evidence. Reuse valid support after
checking its applicability; refresh affected bindings when source, purpose,
facts or default selection changes. Visual documentation is optional.

Codex holds the common pen. Common acceptance and release selection remain the
product owner's decisions; current checkpoint and review state are in Goals.

## Historical publications

the immutable [3.0.5-rc.1 cut](https://github.com/foolishimp/specification_methodology/releases/tag/specification_methodology%2Fv3.0.5-rc.1) and five corresponding Product selectors.

explicit reset; its evidence remains preserved. This publication changes no

Source SHA-256: fb5529c530d48cf45fc8f60ba97fe4885726680fbec9ff2641a26a4a4ca151fb

[Original lines 1-53]
# Intent

## Executive Summary

The Specification Methodology governs construction from the bound current state A to an acceptable outcome Z under finite resources. It defines the required conditions, selects and revises a strategy, and establishes what the resulting work satisfies. Its [universal construction model](PRODUCT.md#universal-construction-model) defines A and Z and applies to software, human processes and mixed realisations. It reconstructs accumulated practice around [shared principles and laws](PRODUCT.md#foundational-reduction), reducing independent interpretation and reconciliation while preserving necessary distinctions.

Z is a set of constraints on acceptable outcomes. The specification supplies the internal reference model of what must hold; the workspace and material external observations supply evidence of what does hold. A path from A to Z may need to be discovered through bounded construction and evaluation. Feedback compares observed conditions with the model, diagnoses discrepancies and directs authorised correction. Bugs and new features enter the same work process. The workspace persists while an actor's account of it can be discarded and rebuilt.

Strategies such as steel thread, phased delivery and repeated refinement arrange the work and its assurance differently. Their selection considers resources, time to useful feedback, risks and expected rework. Preservation through repeated steps, progress and eventual completion are separate claims. Evidence and authority determine whether to continue, revise, repair, recover or stop with an explicit unresolved outcome.

Construction and tests can follow parallel derivations from shared obligations. Their [mutual assurance](requirements/ASSURANCE.md) depends on what each assertion means, how its observations support a conclusion and which errors the paths can share. Formal preservation obligations connect local checks to the outcome; agreement alone proves neither the checks' fidelity to those obligations nor satisfaction of the required outcome. After a change, the method reassesses affected claims and retains evidence whose applicability is established. Required assurance governs this selection within the chosen risk and resource limits.

Finite context and attention require task-specific reference frames: sufficient relevant material, governing rules and authority for a task. Roles and coordination structures follow from the work, actor capabilities and required assurance. Each realisation justifies its arrangement.

The product consists of method documents and their interpretation in the axiomatic calculus, a_c. [Foundations](PRODUCT.md#method-structure) own shared laws; the Spec Method applies them to defining, constructing, evaluating and releasing products; profiles add domain-specific rules. Shared meaning has one owner. Evaluations use one result algebra that preserves failures, uncertainty and domain distinctions.

The method relates what an algorithm does, what its information means and how work is carried out. Category theory states these relationships with explicit preservation obligations; each categorical claim requires the declared demonstration. Each [build tenant](PRODUCT.md#build-tenant-binding) binds common requirements to a computational calculus and a human, machine or mixed execution environment. The a_c interpretation compresses the method's logic into constraints and dependencies an actor can acquire for its task, preserving permitted choices and unknowns. Its fidelity and usefulness require evidence from the represented rules and actual use.

## Mission

To define a precise method for choosing and carrying out lawful construction from A to Z under finite resources, using a coherent core of principles expressed as constraints within which people and computational actors can work. The method uses categorical relationships and an axiomatic representation to make its logic explicit, check its constructions and apply the method to itself.

The method defines the current state A, the constraints on an acceptable destination Z, and the transitions that can carry work between them. Intent, Product and Specification refine the destination; construction and evaluation establish whether it has been reached. Strategy allocates finite resources among understanding, planning, realisation, observation and assurance. The method separates what must be achieved from the choices made to realise it. Universal scope supplies a common construction account across domains; reachability, optimality and correctness remain claims requiring their own support.

## What The Product Is

The product is a standalone body of method documents and their formal representation in the axiomatic calculus, a_c. It reduces the accumulated method to the principles and shared laws needed to define, construct, evaluate and release a product. Earlier rules and experience supply obligations and counterexamples; their previous document boundaries do not determine the successor's architecture.

The [foundational pillars](PRODUCT.md#foundational-reduction) define the contributions that the reconstruction preserves and connects. They govern the rules used to make decisions throughout the method. The algebraic and computational calculi are parts of this core; neither accounts for the whole method alone.

Foundations state the shared commitments from which the method is built. Its deciding rules identify their foundational premises, the consequences that follow, and any additional domain or policy choices. Shared laws replace repeated determinations in the consumers that use them. The Spec Method applies those commitments through Intent, Product, Specification and Realisation. Profiles specialise the method for particular kinds of construction. Their roles are defined in [Product](PRODUCT.md#method-structure).

Shared meaning has one owner. The Spec Method and profiles use the shared contracts where work is performed and evaluated. Results use one algebra: a common set of outcomes and rules for combining them. Its refinements preserve domain meaning, failures and uncertainty. The precise obligations are [single ownership](requirements/README.md#single-ownership-requirement) and the [single result algebra](requirements/README.md#single-result-algebra-requirement).

## Direction

Category theory supplies a precise language for objects, transformations and their composition. The method distinguishes three accounts of a construction: what an algorithm does, what its information means, and how people, machines or combinations of them carry out the work. A mapping between accounts states what it preserves and how that preservation is established. Categorical claims require the [declared demonstration](requirements/README.md#categorical-demonstration-standard); claims without it remain hypotheses.

Axiomatic programming expresses the laws and constraints that permitted constructions satisfy. The a_c interpretation compresses the prose's logic into explicit objects, constraints and dependencies, retaining permitted choices and unresolved conditions. These form a [constraint surface](requirements/README.md#constraint-surfaces-and-logic-compression): an actor can determine what governs its work while choosing a solution within the remaining freedom. A compressed representation must preserve the distinctions needed for that determination.

Refinement adds detail within those obligations until a realisation can be built and evaluated. Each [build tenant](PRODUCT.md#build-tenant-binding) binds the common requirements to a computational calculus and its concrete execution environment, including human processes. The binding establishes how the realisation preserves the required meaning and constraints. The axiomatic representation distinguishes foundational premises, derived rules and declared choices; the tenant supplies the execution binding. Mechanical structure checks, mathematical arguments, semantic assessment and evidence from use support their respective claims.

The method applies its own construction and evaluation process to its successor. [Categorical Construction](PRODUCT.md#categorical-construction) defines how a bounded example tests the direction and how its findings feed back into the method.

## What It Achieves

Actors have finite context and finite attention. Context limits what an activation can hold; attention limits what it can effectively use for its task. Context management selects a sufficient reference frame: the task's purpose, subject, governing rules, required material, authority and result, within the actor's capability. It retains material dependencies and excludes irrelevant material. Work that exceeds one frame is refined into bounded transitions, with lawful continuation or an explicit stop when necessary.

Task dependencies, actor capabilities and required assurance constrain how work and information are distributed across frames. Roles and coordination structures emerge from that allocation. Different configurations can satisfy the same outcome; they can adapt as work and capabilities change within the governing constraints. The [frame requirement](requirements/README.md#bounded-transitions-and-reference-frames) connects these choices to context, attention, authority and evidence. Each realisation explains its configuration rather than assuming a fixed inventory of roles.

The workspace persists while each actor acquires a bounded account of it. That account can be discarded and rebuilt against current reality. Resumption uses the current workspace as the next starting state and retains valid progress. Recorded results are reusable while they remain applicable and their validity conditions hold. Changed dependencies and observations that invalidate a claim reopen the affected work. A change to the destination follows its owning authority and re-entry rules.

Producing a candidate, checking it and accepting it remain distinct acts. Results record their basis, evidence and unresolved matters so that later work can use them without depending on an earlier actor's private memory. The released method [stands alone](requirements/README.md#standalone-successor-requirement), with the predecessor's material claims accounted for under the [disposition requirement](requirements/README.md#successor-disposition-requirement).

===== END SOURCE =====

Source SHA-256: e06f7d704c58397e843cfd1a8720ba878e050c6bab6c5ec8d90317b092b604fc

# Product

## Product Boundary

The product is a specification methodology. It consists of the documents stating its rules for defining, building, checking and releasing a product, together with their formal interpretation in the axiomatic calculus, a_c. The [Intent](INTENT.md) states its purpose; [Method Structure](#method-structure) defines its parts. Each document of the method is a member.

The method's earlier releases are named STDO, short for Specification, Ticketing, Design and Outcome-Driven Development. The prefix remains in identifiers: a `stdo://` address names a file inside an installed release, which the installed toolchain resolves to the read-only copy on this machine, and rule IDs such as STDO-UP-015 name rules of that release.

The original v3 reconstruction succeeded the method's 2.5.1-rc.2 release. That immutable predecessor, `stdo://releases/v2.5.1-rc.2/` (manifest SHA-256 `3d860ff4c1746f06ac25295a9e205cffb8e7725869615ac77cf2304b70ff2782`), governed that construction. Its C3 contracts and evidence retain that historical basis. Current development uses the builder selected below.

A predecessor claim is a rule or statement in the previous release that could affect the new one. Every predecessor claim is listed in [requirements/PREDECESSOR_CLAIMS.md](requirements/PREDECESSOR_CLAIMS.md). Each receives exactly one disposition: `conserved`, `superseded`, `intentionally removed` or `not applicable`. The previous release's own rule [STDO-UP-015](stdo://releases/v2.5.1-rc.2/standards/RELEASE_METHOD.md#successor-baseline-conservation-stdo-up-015) requires this before a successor is accepted.

Under the [standalone successor requirement](requirements/README.md#standalone-successor-requirement), the product establishes its foundational commitments and reconstructs the method on them. Restatement requires foundation-to-law, ownership-and-use, standalone conservation and demonstrated reduction. Unchanged text is permitted when those relationships hold. The new release is complete without the previous release in force. Its size follows the obligations it must express; retaining an old member's layout or adding an explanatory layer does not establish reduction.

Each rule has one owning section, under [Method Structure](#method-structure) and the [single ownership requirement](requirements/README.md#single-ownership-requirement).

The complete five-Product [3.0.5-rc.1 cohort](requirements/COMPLETE_RELEASE.md)
is a historical distribution subject on public version line STDO 3.0.5.
Its owner reset grant and custody remain in
[the reset activation](../.ai-workspace/work/STDO305_RC_RESET/release-tools/ACTIVATION.json),
and its publication status is recorded in the original release evidence.
Current source development follows the separately owned
[Product tool packages](../development/TOOLS.md), including the independent
Purpose Context Product and Skills consumption. No successor release constructor
or qualification is declared. The adopted builder remains exact STDO 3.0.4.

The preceding distribution increment, STDO 3.0.4, is published under the owner's
[release selection](../.ai-workspace/work/STDO304_DELIVERY/SELECTION.md); the
[release record](../.ai-workspace/work/STDO304_DELIVERY/RELEASED.md) binds its
exact publication, public installation and closed independent review.
It adds the reviewed [coordinated skills](requirements/COORDINATED_SKILLS.md),
[review-flow amendment](requirements/REVIEW_FLOW.md) and bounded reader repairs
to the published 3.0.3 payload. Release integrity is checked separately from the
retained, limited native workflow qualification. Consumer selections and the
independently owned a_c/Skills source bundle remain unchanged.

The preceding 3.0.3 distribution introduced
[installed interactive documentation](requirements/INSTALLED_DOCUMENTATION.md)
under [T-006](../.ai-workspace/tickets/active/T-006-STDO-3.0.3-documentation.md).
The constraint explorer is a required documentation asset. Packaging preserves
its exact represented source and explanatory standing; it does not turn a
historical graph into the current methodology or a proof of its prose.
The owner-selected [installed tooling](requirements/INSTALLED_TOOLING.md) adds
pinned a_c and Skills source distributions, safe explicit workspace materialization,
the reviewed ABI code example and an offline Lean proof reader. Their existing
Product ownership, execution contracts and bases remain unchanged.

The preceding source increment is
[recursive Product composition](requirements/PRODUCT_COMPOSITION.md), constructed
over the published 3.0.1 baseline. The owner now selects its standalone native
3.0.2 stable release under the [release selection](../.ai-workspace/work/STDO302_DELIVERY/SELECTION.md).
It changes no installed consumer basis. The preceding bounded common successor work is the
[selected 3.0.1 amendment](requirements/STDO_3_0_1.md) under T-004, targeting
`3.0.1-rc.1` from the exact native `3.0.0-rc.1` predecessor it names. That
amendment owns the changed deterministic-kind obligation and compatibility
scope. The historical v3 reconstruction and inherited acceptance duties
below retain their original subjects and standing; their historical builder is unchanged.

The method distinguishes three accounts of a construction. They apply across the tiers:

- the algorithmic description: the steps;
- the informational interpretation: what the data means;
- the computational realisation: how people, machines or combinations of them carry it out in a declared execution environment.

A tier may need all three. A category, equivalence or functor is established only when its claim meets the categorical demonstration standard.

Each candidate supplies and owns its interpretation of the method in a_c. The calculus, interpretation and encoded carrier have distinct content identities and proof boundaries under the [a_c dependency contract](requirements/A_C_DEPENDENCY.md). The Axiom Indexer checker and the existing released STDO Representation are separately owned Products. A candidate's interpretation does not replace either Product or inherit its acceptance.

Runtimes that execute applications built with the method are outside the product. Examples are ABIogenesis (ABG), which runs typed graph programs and records what happened at run time, its executor HoG, which traverses those programs, and application workflow runtimes.

Example applications, such as Hello World and Data Mapper, are test subjects outside the product. So is the sum-and-histogram example used by the steel thread. Their particular features never become general method rules because they were used once.

The product changes nothing in the 2.* line: not its source project, its releases, its companion products or the selection of versions released together. The companion products are the Axiom Indexer; the STDO Representation, which publishes the method's canonical a_c map, linking each axiom to its source text; and the plugin, which carries the method's skills for coding agents.

Construction proceeds through numbered checkpoints, C0 to C3, described under [Checkpoints](#checkpoints). The two candidate build tenants are described under [Source Project Construction](#source-project-construction).

## Foundational Reduction

The method recovers the essential contributions of the following pillars and expresses their shared laws once. These are related accounts of the method, not a prescribed document list, a fixed sequence of work or a claim that each is an independent axiom.

| Pillar | Contribution the reconstruction must account for |
|---|---|
| Specification and project structure | Purpose and destination constraints; refinement from common WHAT to independent HOW realisations; ownership of specification, design, work and commentary. Project structure expresses those relations. |
| World models, identity and Markov objects | Objects, boundaries, state and material relations; the evidence supporting a boundary and a claim that its observed state is sufficient. Ordinary bounded modelling does not establish stronger Markov properties. |
| Algebraic calculus | Objects, typed operations and laws for composing and transforming them. A lawful transformation states what it preserves and the conditions under which it applies. |
| Functional design | Apply typed contracts, composition, invariant preservation and explicit failure and effect boundaries to decomposition. Reuse transformations while retaining each domain's meaning and permissions. |
| Outcome-driven development | Construction through typed transitions toward a selected outcome, with evaluation, continuation, recovery and termination. Operation kind, actor and execution mechanism remain distinct. |
| Information and interpretation | The relationship between a subject, its representations and their meaning; the distinctions a projection, translation or compression must preserve for its consumer. |
| Reference frames | Task-sufficient acquisition under finite context and attention; bounded results and justified allocations of work, information and authority. |
| Computational calculus and execution binding | Describe the operations and composition laws by which work is carried out, including effects and resource measures. Bind that calculus to a concrete human, machine or mixed environment and establish that the binding satisfies its laws. |
| Evidence, correction and authority | Claims distinguished from observations and acceptance; changes driven by counterexamples and traced to their cause; permission and ownership distinct from capability or a successful check. |
| Axiomatic calculus | Explicit, recoverable representation of the method's logical constraints, dependencies, permitted latitude and residual uncertainty, usable to construct the constraint surface for a task. |

The [reduction requirement](requirements/README.md#foundational-reduction-requirement) owns the evidence needed to show how these contributions govern the method's deciding rules. Its arguments identify their applicable [foundational premises](requirements/PRINCIPLES.md), additional domain premises and consuming rules. A pillar names a required contribution, not an additional axiom or a claim of derivation from finite capacity. The [constraint-surface requirement](requirements/README.md#constraint-surfaces-and-logic-compression) owns the relationship between prose, a_c, bounded acquisition and permissible work. Domain-specific policies retain their own premises and owners. Finite resources alone do not supply purpose, identity, authority or compatible composition.

## Construction And Context Management

Context management applies the [universal construction model](#universal-construction-model) to the material and responsibilities needed for each bounded action. The account of A distinguishes material workspace state from each actor's acquired view of it. The workspace is the continuing truth surface for construction; material external state is observed through its owning interfaces. Context is acquired under the [bounded-transition and frame requirement](requirements/README.md#bounded-transitions-and-reference-frames). Unknowns remain explicit.

When work exceeds one activation's capacity, decomposition bounds the selected actions and their material joins under the [construction requirement](requirements/README.md#universal-construction-requirement). A sequence A → B → C → … describes successive states; Z remains the constraints on an acceptable outcome. Independent actions may proceed concurrently where their dependencies, effects and authority permit.

Finite context and finite attention drive context management. Context bounds what an activation can hold; attention bounds what it can use effectively for its selected task. A reference frame selects sufficient relevant material and the task contract within those limits. Time, spend and other resources constrain the selected execution binding as applicable. The [bounded-transition and frame requirement](requirements/README.md#bounded-transitions-and-reference-frames) owns these obligations.

Roles and coordination structures emerge from allocating required operations and information to actors. Task dependencies require exchanges and compatible results; actor fallibility and incomplete knowledge motivate the applicable checking and recovery. Limited context and attention bound each responsibility. These conditions constrain a configuration without uniquely determining it. Functional decomposition, communication, authority and evaluation dependency remain distinct relations.

A realisation may use a hierarchy, peer coordination or an arrangement that adapts as actors allocate work within their contracts. Its design relates that choice to the task, actor capabilities, communication costs and required assurance. Several configurations may be adequate; an optimality claim is relative to stated objectives, constraints and comparison scope. Operational profiles and build tenants own these choices. The [construction binding](#construction-authority) selects one configuration for building this release.

## Universal Construction Model

The method organises construction around the problem of reaching an acceptable outcome from current reality under finite resources and incomplete knowledge. A is the current state bound for the selected construction. Its account describes material workspace state, relevant external observations, justified knowledge and explicit unknowns; an actor's acquired account can be incomplete or stale. Z defines the required conditions on acceptable outcomes, including acceptance conditions, and can be expressed as a predicate; several realised states may satisfy it. The governing specification is the selected WHAT across Intent, Product and Specification, whose descriptions refine Z. A selected construction may need to discover its path while performing and evaluating work.

The applicable declared requirements and computational calculi govern lawful transformations and compositions under the [build-tenant binding](#build-tenant-binding). The governing specification supplies the chosen outcome constraints; the calculi do not select them. Strategy chooses work within the laws, execution produces a result, and evidence establishes what that result satisfies. A lawful construction can remain incomplete or fail to achieve its selected outcome. Conditional proofs about that outcome retain their premises and scope.

The specification is the internal reference model of required conditions. Observations describe reality. Causal or predictive models explain how an intervention may change it. These accounts have different purposes and can be incomplete or wrong in different ways. Diagnosis follows the [construction requirement](requirements/README.md#universal-construction-requirement), distinguishing the realisation, governing requirement or design, predictive model and observation.

Construction combines admissible transformations under compatible contracts, preserving the applicable outer obligations. Strategy selects their decomposition, order, concurrency and assurance within those constraints. Steel thread, waterfall and iterative delivery are possible strategies. Each can have different resource costs, feedback delays, integration risks and rework. Their selection belongs to the realisation and the actor exercising the admitted work authority; a claim of superiority states the objectives, comparison and assumptions on which it depends.

Feedback connects observation, comparison, causal diagnosis, action selection, execution and renewed observation. Homeostasis here means correction toward required conditions as disturbances and new observations arise; it guarantees neither convergence nor the adequacy of those conditions. For work management, a bug is a category of feature/change work: an observed or suspected discrepancy handled through the same intake and delivery mechanism. Diagnosis determines whether to repair the realisation, acquire evidence or revise the governing model through its owner.

Recursive construction, repeated correction and ongoing operation distinguish preservation, progress and completion. A correct local step does not establish a successful composition or eventual arrival. Proof, testing and observed use support their declared claims; an unavailable proof remains an explicit assurance obligation where that proof is required. A resource limit can end an attempt with an unresolved outcome. It cannot establish either achievement or impossibility.

The [assurance requirement](requirements/ASSURANCE.md) gives this model its formal preservation, assertion and observation obligations. Construction and assurance can follow parallel paths from the same required meaning. Their comparison accounts for shared causes of error and the scope of each conclusion. Changes trigger reassessment through material validity dependencies; complete assurance can combine applicable retained evidence with new evaluation. Foundations own these shared relationships, and the Spec Method applies them at testing, triage, recovery and release consumers under the selected risk and resource policy.

This model connects the existing [principles](requirements/PRINCIPLES.md#universal-construction-synthesis) and [pillars](#foundational-reduction). Foundations own its shared state, composition, resource, evidence and control relationships; the Spec Method applies them to delivery and correction; profiles and build tenants supply their domain policies and execution bindings. The [universal construction requirement](requirements/README.md#universal-construction-requirement) owns its acceptance obligations. Universal scope establishes no general solver, fixed organisational structure, guaranteed convergence or theorem of categorical universality.

## Method Structure

The method has three tiers of method law. The [C3 responsibility table](#c3-construction-contract) identifies their obligations and predecessor coverage inputs. Guides and packaging have supporting responsibilities; the conventions and formats they own bind work within their declared scope.

| Tier | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| Foundations | Shared commitments and contracts for interpretation, purpose, identity, composition, finite context and attention, evidence, authority, results and reference frames, connected by the universal construction model. |
| Spec Method | Product definition, requirements, construction, assurance, change, re-entry and release, applying the foundational contracts through the same delivery and correction process. |
| Profiles | Domain or operational specialisations that import the shared contracts and own their additional bindings. |

The Spec Method develops a product through four descriptions:

- Intent states why it exists and the outcomes it must achieve.
- Product defines what it is, its parts and boundaries.
- Specification states its required behaviour, constraints and checks.
- Realisation supplies design, implementation and evidence against the specification.

Each refinement preserves the obligations above it. A material constituent repeats this structure where it needs its own bounded account. Work records, triage and review connect changes to the earliest affected description.

Profiles include outcome-driven development of typed graph programs, user-interface construction from typed state and messages, and world modelling over authoritative source systems. Operational profiles specialise the reference-frame contract through task catalogues and justified coordination arrangements. Guides own writing, posting and glossary conventions; packaging carries the method's members and entry points.

The a_c interpretation represents the method's laws, results and declared maps as a recoverable logic compression, retaining premises, dependencies, consequences, policy choices and unresolved conditions. Reference-frame projections select the governing constraint surface and material for a task from their owners. Interpretation and projection apply across the tiers; neither creates a second rule owner or an execution grant. Their fidelity follows the constraint-surface requirement; task sufficiency is evaluated through criterion 4 of the [C3 contract](#c3-construction-contract).

## Build-Tenant Binding

A build tenant binds the common requirements calculus to a computational calculus grounded in a real-world execution environment. It owns the design, realisation and evidence for that binding. The [binding requirement](requirements/README.md#build-tenant-binding-requirement) governs its conformance.

The requirements calculus comprises the obligations, permitted outcomes and rules for refining and composing the construction. The computational calculus describes executable operations, their composition and permitted transformations, with the effects and resource behaviour relevant to those obligations. Its execution environment supplies the concrete means: a technology stack, a human process or a combination. A human process can use people, roles, documents, communication channels and decision procedures.

The common specification bounds the permitted realisations. Each tenant selects its computational calculus and execution binding within those bounds. The algorithmic, informational and computational accounts apply across this relation. Formal representation in a_c supports checking the relation; it does not select its executor. A tenant's author and the actors that carry out its realised process are separate coordinates.

## Product Identity And Lineage

| Coordinate | Binding |
|---|---|
| Product line | the Specification Methodology, whose 2.* source project is `specification_methodology/v2/` (`urn:stdo:product-definition:specification-methodology`) and whose release namespace is `specification_methodology` |
| Release | successor releases on that line, in the same namespace; current release work follows [RELEASE](stdo://releases/v3.0.4/standards/RELEASE.md), while historical cuts retain their original release basis |
| Reconstruction predecessor | `stdo://releases/v2.5.1-rc.2/`, immutable; current development basis is declared above |
| Source project | `specification_methodology/v3/`, beside the 2.* source project, which it does not overwrite |
| Identifiers | `urn:stdo:product-definition:stdo-3`, `urn:stdo-3:bounded-context:method` and the `urn:stdo-3:` tenant identities are identifiers, not names. They do not change with the directory or the display name. |

[stdo_3.json](../stdo_3.json) is the machine-readable Product Definition that the installed release's tools read to locate this source project. It carries this identity in its product name and in a local axiom that points to this section. A local axiom is a rule this source project adds on top of the installed release's law. Its schema has no version field.

## Result Algebra

The Foundations own the method's single result algebra: one set of outcome values, and one way of combining them, in which every result is reported. The following are stated in it or as refinements of it:

- constraint checks;
- review judgements;
- reference-frame evaluations;
- stops;
- work steps;
- effects (changes made to files or systems).

No member, profile or frame defines its own parallel set of evaluation or decision outcomes. A refinement gives finer outcomes for one domain. Its owner binds the predicate and the conditions that determine each value's meaning, keeps the original domain outcome alongside the core value, and never raises the result's standing. Recorded states remain distinct from judgments about those states. The precise obligation is the [single result algebra requirement](requirements/README.md#single-result-algebra-requirement). The [conformance discriminators](conformance/README.md), the common test cases every member is checked against, check it in every member under acceptance criterion 5 of the [C3 Construction Contract](#c3-construction-contract).

## Categorical Construction

Categorical construction is a general capability of the method. The method defines categories of its own constructs and categorical joins, which are maps from one category to another. These maps are distinct from the composition boundaries between successive construction steps. It permits a construction step only where declared laws allow it. Specification documents are the objects. Derivations and refinements are the arrows, and each states what it must preserve. A join of an algorithm category with a computation category is the acceptance instance for the capability. The [categorical demonstration standard](requirements/README.md#categorical-demonstration-standard) states what each categorical claim must show; a claim without it is a hypothesis.

[CAPABILITIES](requirements/CAPABILITIES.md) owns the acceptance contract required for every selected capability. This capability's contract is derived from the steel thread's evidence at step 3 below. It has not been selected yet: the product owner has not chosen it for current work.

The following sequence is this source project's strategy for establishing the categorical capability. Consumers select their own strategy under the universal construction model; activation of this project's steel thread remains a separate owner decision.

Sequence:

1. The Intent and this Product state the categorical direction, the axiomatic representation of requirements, the [build-tenant binding](#build-tenant-binding) to computation and concrete execution, and the demonstration standard.
2. One thin end-to-end example, the steel thread, tests the direction under the installed previous release.
3. The method is corrected from the steel thread's evidence.
   - Each finding goes back to the section that actually owns its cause.
   - A failure counts against the method only when triangulation places the cause in the method. For this test, triangulation applies [causal diagnosis](requirements/README.md#universal-construction-requirement) through several reference frames over the same subject.
   - Corrected clauses are reviewed independently.
   - The standalone successor requirement, member acceptance and release obligations stay in force.
   - The steel thread supports its own join only. It is not evidence that the whole method works.
4. The full categorical product is built on the released method.

Steel thread. Its subject is the sum-and-histogram example. A list of numbers is split across partitions. The example computes their exact sum, and an approximate quantile (such as the 95th percentile) from histograms built per partition. It belongs to no project, it stays outside the method's own members, and it runs under the installed release. Its minimum contract:

1. A stated purpose and the meaning of the inputs and results. The exact law for the sum. For the approximate quantile: the adequacy relation (how close is close enough), the quantile convention, how the buckets cover the range, and the output rule. The failure outcomes that matter.
2. The actual objects and arrows of the example's categories, when two arrows are equal, identity and composition, and each specific map claimed. Each is related to the algorithmic, informational and computational accounts.
3. One exact rewrite that splits the input and combines the partial results (partition and reduction). One bounded approximation. Each has explicit conditions and an argument that the end-to-end result is adequate.
4. Execution and comparison:
   - an exact oracle, a reference computation specified independently of the one under test;
   - two concrete execution bindings, which are two ways of running the computation;
   - a comparison of returned meaning, required effects and diagnostics;
   - a refused-input case and an interrupted or uncertain-effect case, run beside normal completion.
5. Measured latency, bytes moved and memory over a declared workload, with the measurement boundaries stated. The context, time and spend limits of the run that executes it are recorded. Only the cost and generality claims those measurements support are made.
6. The steel thread's own chain from Intent through Product and Specification to Realisation. Each derivation step is an arrow whose preservation obligation is stated and discharged. A failed derivation is attributed under step 3 and counts against the method only when triangulation places the cause in the method.

Reference design. The [category-of-algorithms design](../.ai-workspace/comments/claude/assets/20261001T133903Z_STRATEGY_a-category-of-algorithms-with-computation.compressed.md) is a reference design and the steel thread's subject matter. It is read with the [corrections](../.ai-workspace/comments/claude/20261013T012745Z_STRATEGY_categorical_groundwork.md#corrections-to-the-attached-design) in the categorical groundwork strategy. Its five layers are not method rules. They are: the domain calculus (algorithms and their laws); the computational algebra (distributed operations and the cost grade on each arrow); the computational calculus (lawful plans and rewrites); the workload (volumes, arrival rates and service levels); and the binding to a technology stack and operational model.

Three further ideas from that design stay unresolved until a real example needs them and supports them:

- the sheaf account, which says which computations can be split across partitions and glued back;
- a universal grade algebra, which is one way of composing every cost measure on an arrow (latency, bytes moved, memory, width);
- generality across a second domain.

## Dependencies And Acceptance

[The a_c dependency contract](requirements/A_C_DEPENDENCY.md) fixes the exact a_c theory used for construction and checking, and the exact, unchangeable Axiom Indexer release used for mechanical checks. The released interpretation's encoding basis follows the separate identity and packaging decision. The neighbouring Axiom Indexer Product Definition only says where to find that product; it does not fix the version.

The [conformance discriminators](conformance/README.md#member-conformance) supply the applicable checks for each member. The separate construction and integrated-closure section checks the Executive's acceptance decisions and C3 closure. None of the following shows that a member's meaning is acceptable now: passing syntax checks, valid maps, the number of documents, a local approval or an earlier passing run.

## Source Project Construction

This source project is where the next release is written. It owns one common WHAT: the specification in `specification/`, which states what the product must be.

Two build tenants supply the HOW, authored respectively by Codex and Claude. Each supplies its own candidate of the product as documents under `build_tenants/<tenant>/`, applying the [build-tenant binding requirement](requirements/README.md#build-tenant-binding-requirement). Author identity alone selects no execution environment for the method the candidate expresses.

For this document construction, each tenant applies the [build-tenant binding requirement](requirements/README.md#build-tenant-binding-requirement) to its own method-authoring process in its existing design and work records, under the installed builder. Its candidate also states the binding rule that future consumers must apply. The candidate's rule does not replace the builder governing its own construction.

Their organisation, wording and a_c encodings may differ. Their accepted outputs satisfy the same common contract and the same independently specified expectations. The method a tenant's documents express is distinct from this common specification, which governs how they are built. A tenant design does not replace this Product definition, and the common WHAT is not replaced by a merge of the tenants' work. Selecting or combining tenant candidates for a release is the product owner's decision.

### Construction Authority

The common construction pen edits the common specification and records. Common changes receive review independent of their author before the product owner accepts them. Checkpoint commits on main provide review visibility; they do not supply acceptance. A review result informs the product owner's decision. The [frame basis](REFERENCE_FRAME_BASIS.md) binds current actors and activations under the adopted 3.0.4 release, with the historical C3 configuration separately preserved.

Work is done in activations. An activation binds a capable actor to a selected reference frame, its subject and admitted authority. This source project's task-based construction separates two attention needs: carrying out a bounded change and managing delivery across successive tasks. The Executive retains the longer-term outcome and uses a sufficient bounded view of worker results to decide what follows. It requests further bounded evaluation when a decision needs more information. Independent assessment provides the checking required by this construction contract. These needs explain the selected responsibilities below; their named arrangement is one configuration under the installed builder:

- an Executive frame selects the next work and disposes results within its decision grant, with no file or Git mutation;
- a Worker frame constructs a bounded result; the Writer frame used here specialises it for changes to documents and records in an exact granted territory;
- a Reviewer frame assesses a frozen subject against the applicable contract, with independently acquired evidence, no repair grant and no assessment of its own authorship.

These frames organise context and attention for their respective tasks. Their names confer no authority. The existing grants below and in each activation supply the permitted effects and decisions.


[Original lines 302-407]
### Development Isolation

These local development rules preserve the original tenant reconstruction isolation contract. The common pen still owns common source changes on `main`, and a separately activated Writer performs effects. New work binds its exact territory and carrier under the [current development basis](#current-development-basis); the historical C3-specific ticket, snapshot and tenant clauses below are not an automatic new activation. They change no installed rule or release cut.

- Lineage. `main` is the sole lineage of this source project. The common
  WHAT (`specification/` and the Product Definition) and common work records
  are written on `main` by the common pen. An authorised Reviewer writes its
  own review under `.ai-workspace/comments/<reviewer>/`; this grants no
  change to the common definition, tickets or another reviewer's text. Frozen reviews are
  retained as written; a later review or response names its own subject.
- Tenant branches. Each build tenant works on a short-lived topic branch
  `tenant/<tenant>/<checkpoint>` cut from `main`, in its own git worktree,
  with a sparse checkout limited to `specification_methodology/v3/specification/`,
  `specification_methodology/v3/.ai-workspace/` and its own
  `specification_methodology/v3/build_tenants/<tenant>/`. The
  peer tenant's directory is not present in that worktree.
- Territory. A tenant commits only under its own `build_tenants/<tenant>/`.
  A needed common change is requested from the pen and reaches the tenant by
  merging `main` inward. A tenant does not edit tickets, common files or the
  peer tenant.
- Current work carrier. Use [TICKETS §5](stdo://releases/v3.0.4/standards/TICKETS.md#5-from-carrier-to-execution) and the exact current grant to select and admit a run-scoped execution contract.
- Historical C3 work carrier. The then-installed
  [Work-Carrier-To-Execution Rule](stdo://releases/v2.5.1-rc.2/standards/TICKET_METHOD.md#work-carrier-to-execution-rule)
  requires every run to execute under one run-scoped execution contract,
  derived from an existing ticket.
  - C3 work is carried by one upstream authority ticket and one tenant copy
    per build tenant under `.ai-workspace/tickets/`.
  - Each member activation runs under an execution contract derived from the
    tenant ticket. The pen drafts the contract on the Executive's selection,
    and the contract is admitted as a separate step: the pen records the
    Executive's decision to run it in the ticket, with the contract's
    identity and digest, before execution. It is not a further
    ticket.
  - The ticket has one writing owner, the common pen, acting as a Writer
    activation in common territory. Before the next activation starts, the
    pen records, each as the Executive decided it: the admitted contract, the
    closed Writer result, the Reviewer result, the triage, the Executive's
    disposition and the next authorised activation.
  - The Executive activation decides and holds no write grant to tickets,
    common files, candidates or worktrees.
  - The tenant Writer's return is written in its own territory
    (`build_tenants/<tenant>/qualification/`) and never in the ticket.
  - Within that historical C3 contract, the ticket is the only record that can authorise an action. No C3 Writer
    activation starts without one, and no C3 disposition exists until the pen has recorded it.
- Inputs. Tenant worktrees use sparse-checkout cone mode. A declared shared
  input outside the cone is read from the snapshot's input commit with
  `git show <input-commit>:<path>`, never from a working tree. A
  file absent from the cone is neither licence to read elsewhere nor
  evidence that the declared input is absent.
- Snapshot. A checkpoint input snapshot names the input commit on `main`
  whose tree holds every member, and it records each member's hash as read
  from that commit. The ticket entry that records the snapshot is a later,
  descendant commit, so the record is not part of the snapshot it records.
- Review. The product owner authorises reviews between tenants. A reviewer
  reads the reviewed tenant's branch at a named commit, never its working
  tree, and writes the review under `.ai-workspace/comments/<reviewer>/`.
  Review authority grants no candidate or peer-tenant write access.
- Merge. A tenant branch merges to `main` only on the product owner's
  acceptance of the checkpoint review, or an explicit owner-directed variance
  recorded in the ticket. A visibility merge supplies no acceptance. The branch is deleted and its
  worktree removed after merge. An unmerged tenant branch is work in
  progress, not a lineage; `git worktree prune` runs only after merge.
- Porting reads. Porting is copying a part of the peer tenant's candidate
  into one's own. Each tenant's Writer and Reviewer activations may read the
  peer tenant's candidate at the donor commit named in its admitted contract
  under [Cross-Synthesis](#cross-synthesis), for porting and comparison. Ported
  text is written only in the porting tenant's own territory.
- Release cuts. `rc/*` and `release/*` branches are not tenant branches. Historical cuts retain their selected RELEASE_METHOD basis; current release work uses the adopted [RELEASE](stdo://releases/v3.0.4/standards/RELEASE.md) owner.

### Cross-Synthesis

Construction has three stages: ratify one common specification, synthesise two candidates on that specification, then compare them for the product owner's selection of the next basis. Codex holds the common pen. Each tenant keeps its own candidate line and borrows applicable contributions from the other; neither line is rebuilt on the other candidate. The ticket names the exact preceding candidate and donor for each line. Those coordinates are construction inputs, not product identity.

The common pen classifies each reported divergence against its owning requirement before revising it: implementation defect, permitted design choice, specification gap or reserved owner decision. A required correction becomes tenant work; a permitted choice remains available. A common change receives independent review and the product owner's ratification at an exact commit before dependent tenant contracts are admitted. Each resulting tenant task, or the reason no change is needed, is recorded in the existing work carrier. A revised requirement alone repairs no candidate.

A peer contribution enters a line inside a declared frontier cone. Its record names the frozen source, the receiving owner, any adaptation, the predecessor claims and material dependencies it affects, and the evidence that must be checked again. A proposed port list is input to that selection, not an obligation to copy an unsuitable design. The Reviewer compares the received contribution with its frozen source, the predecessor claims it carries and the receiving line's consumers. The receiving line preserves one owner for each determination and the [single result algebra](#result-algebra). Moving a rule without changing its meaning can conserve it; changed meaning requires the replacement decision under the [disposition requirement](requirements/README.md#successor-disposition-requirement). A frontier records proposals and evidence; acceptance standing comes only from the ticket.

Porting does not erase authorship. The receiving line's same-family nonauthor Reviewer supplies the independent assessment of a ported contribution, with the reason for this model-family exception and its limits recorded. The donor's cross-family reading of its own contribution is supplementary. Other parts receive cross-family review by default under the [independence rule](#c3-construction-contract). Each handoff identifies which review covers each contribution; the contributor cannot supply its own acceptance evidence.

A repair produces a successor freeze. Evidence reuse follows [change impact and targeted reassessment](requirements/ASSURANCE.md#change-impact-and-targeted-reassessment). Its return identifies affected owners, consumer bindings, interpretations, frames, dispositions and evidence, using the existing dependency records. The reviewer checks the changed material and its material dependents, including whether reused evidence still applies. A changed port or invalidated port assessment receives nonauthor readback on the successor; the donor's own reading cannot replace it. Unaffected evidence retains its original scope. No fixed number of repair rounds makes unresolved work complete.

### Candidate Comparison

The [mutual-assurance contract](requirements/ASSURANCE.md#parallel-derivation-and-mutual-assurance) governs what agreement and disagreement between the candidates can establish. Shared sources and received contributions remain material dependencies; actor diversity alone establishes no independent error model.

Both candidates bind the same ratified common input. Each is assessed against the requirements, criteria and discriminators in its admitted input; a later draft does not rebind it. Evidence from different inputs retains its original scope, and obligations present in only one input are reported as unmatched. Qualified selection still requires both candidates to bind the same ratified input. Each keeps its own preceding-candidate comparison under the [reduction requirement](requirements/README.md#foundational-reduction-requirement); the head-to-head comparison uses shared tasks and acquisition conditions rather than treating unlike historical trials as matched evidence.

Before the comparison, a nonauthor specifies the tasks, supplied facts, grants, expected distinctions, ordinary entry routes, tools and resource envelopes; the common pen records them in one shared protocol in common territory, named by the common work carrier and pinned to the same immutable version by both candidates. Expected answers are withheld from task actors. Candidate-specific routing and implementation remain available within those conditions. Curated-frame use and ordinary discovery are reported separately; one cannot stand in for the other. Reuse of an earlier trial requires demonstrated applicability to the shared protocol and current candidate. Changed tasks, additional hints and different acquisition limits remain separate evidence.

Each return accounts for all six member criteria and the [construction and integrated-closure checks](conformance/README.md#construction-and-integrated-closure), distinguishing satisfied, falsified and unresolved obligations. Coverage of a local claim does not establish the whole candidate. Missing assessment withholds the dependent acceptance; it does not invent a failed evaluation of the text. Criterion 4 requires actual use under its declared conditions, including supported context fit. Neither a desk trace nor an unmeasured successful response completes that criterion.

Comparable coverage means the same obligations and material cases are assessed on both candidates under the same criteria, discriminators and, for actual use, the shared protocol; unmatched or unassessed scope is reported separately. Selection on conformance requires the six criteria and integrated obligations to be satisfied on the exact candidate. The owner compares remaining non-blocking findings only over comparable coverage, with their impact and any readability or maintainability tradeoff stated. If neither candidate qualifies, the return is no qualified choice; further construction or a changed selection boundary requires the owner's decision. Choosing a basis does not itself accept unresolved predecessor replacements, publish a release, merge a tenant branch or authorise adoption. Those acts retain their existing owners and conditions.

### Checkpoints

The original reconstruction proceeds through these historical checkpoints:

- C0, structure and bootstrap (setting up this source project and the instruction files that route agents to it): closed;
- C1, principles: closed;
- C2, capabilities and conformance: closed;
- C3: builds each tenant's candidate under the [C3 Construction Contract](#c3-construction-contract).

The state of the closed checkpoints is in [GOALS](GOALS.md).

In C3, members are restated under Executive coordination and evaluated independently. Their frames are tested by fresh actors in actual use, and their a_c checks are selected separately under the [a_c dependency contract](requirements/A_C_DEPENDENCY.md). Release, and adoption (a project choosing to install and use the release), are later explicit decisions over an exact accepted set of members and its dependencies.


[Original lines 501-511]
## Open Owner Decisions

The reconstruction decisions below retain their recorded C3 subjects and standing. Later release records resolve only their explicitly selected scope; this builder adoption does not close historical qualification or acceptance obligations. New decisions use the current development basis.

- Acceptance-review activations for each tenant in C3 remain owner decisions under the review allocation in [Cross-Synthesis](#cross-synthesis). Each ticket records its exact assignment; construction and defect-review authority alone supply no per-member `accept` disposition.
- Actual-use resources for the two-line comparison: the selected actor mechanism, time and spend envelopes. Missing resources leave the affected criterion unresolved; they do not waive it or qualify the other candidate.
- The UX profile's conflicting predecessor compliance tests: which successor determination replaces them and which claims it supersedes. The existing ambiguity rule governs affected construction until that replacement is accepted; a temporary stricter reading is not acceptance of a replacement.
- The steel thread: its selection, its work carrier, and its spend and time limits. The steel thread is step 2 of [Categorical Construction](#categorical-construction).
- Identity and packaging of the new release. The decisions cover whether the compressions are kept, superseded or removed; how identifiers for a_c concepts and for the Traversal Occurrence Profile are issued and accepted into use; and the released interpretation's encoding basis and its standalone acquisition. The successor does not depend on the previous release remaining in force. The relevant decision precedes any dependent member or C3 acceptance and, in all cases, a release-candidate cut.
- Candidate number and release-candidate cut, in the `specification_methodology` release namespace under RELEASE_METHOD. Decided before a release-candidate cut.
- Cohort relation: which versions of the companion products are released together with this one. Decided before a release-candidate cut. Existing Product identifiers and the current cohort are unchanged. The original reconstruction used installed 2.5.1-rc.2; future work uses the adopted 3.0.4 builder.
